| G281 | **A rule about a kind that is absent from the input is not evaluated, and never clean.** The coverage record counted a rule as evaluated when its check ran, whatever it ran over, so `audit -f examples/kube-noob-exceptions.yaml` recorded 87 of 93 rules as evaluated on one ExceptionList and the OSCAL document built from a Deployment-only directory reported 15 controls satisfied; a platform-gated rule read as clean on every bundle of the other platform. Every rule now DECLARES its subject (`subjects=` on `@rule`: a kind, alternatives, the pod-spec kinds, or every object; `context=` for kinds it reads beside the subject), and a rule whose subject is not in the input does not run: `notEvaluated` with the reason "no <Kind> in the input", whatever else it could have read (a rule that reads its context first and its subject second was "evaluated without" under the first, inferred version). A kind counts only in an API group the rules know it in (`SUBJECT_GROUPS`; `by_kind`, `has_subject` and `workloads` filter the same way), so a same-named kind from another group is not the subject and the reason carries the group (`no operators.coreos.com Subscription in the input`). That set is every group the kind has ever been served under, derived from `REMOVED_APIS` (an `extensions/v1beta1` Deployment is a Deployment; the first version refused it, so a legacy bundle lost its findings and gained a false KN-V011 while the Service beside the dropped Deployment was read), and no `apiVersion` at all is an unknown, not a contradiction (API list items carry none, so a live snapshot evaluated 3 rules where 0.9.2 fired 19; every live collector -- posture snapshot, attack-path scan, operator inventory -- stamps the apiVersion the object was listed under through one helper, keeping the backend's own when it has one). OpenShift 3.x's ungrouped `v1` DeploymentConfig, Route, BuildConfig, Build, ImageStream, Template, Project, SecurityContextConstraints and the authorization kinds are read as those kinds (`LEGACY_OPENSHIFT_V1`, from openshift/api release-3.11 `legacy.go`), for exactly those kinds; KN-V002 still fires on them. A workload-kind document in a group the rules do not know is named in the assumptions as not audited, and KN-V011/KN-N012 decline for its namespace rather than conclude "selects nothing" from pods they did not read. KN-V010 declares the four apps kinds it judges and KN-N001 the long-running kinds and Pod it fires for, so neither is evaluated-and-clean on a CronJob-only or Pod-only input; KN-ST001 declares the StorageClass it reads as context, and a test walks every rule body for `by_kind` and fails when the kind is declared as neither; the pod-spec kinds are spelled out inside an alternative label too. The text view's `controls with findings` line names only controls with findings > 0 (it listed every touched control with its count, so a control whose only rule was not evaluated read `AU-2 (0)`), and a `controls not evaluated, in whole or in part` line names each control with a rule not evaluated, the rules, and its clean count when it is partly both, the same set the JSON's `notEvaluated` names. A refused object is never a pass (review 4): an object of a known kind whose apiVersion is not a string, not shaped like an API, or in a group the kind is not known under is listed under `notAudited` with a fixed reason, printed in the text view, carried by the gate's JSON, Markdown ("Not passed: N object(s) not audited"), HTML and SARIF (invocation property plus one `note` result per object) and the snapshot's coverage, the subject reason says `1 Deployment not audited (...)` and never "no pod spec in the input", and `audit` and `gate` exit 1 whatever `--fail-on` says. `apiVersion: null` is a missing apiVersion; a non-string one is refused by shape, never shown as a Python repr; a reason holds no input text (a bidi override in a group name stays out of `why`); KN-V011/KN-N012 decline for pods in a kind with no reader as for a refused workload; OpenShift 3.x bindings' `userNames`/`groupNames` and `SystemUser`/`SystemGroup` subjects are read through the one shared conversion; the declaration walk fails loudly on an unclassifiable traversal; live-snapshot reasons never say "in the input"; KN-V014 keys duplicates on the kind's identity (one object across the groups a kind was served under, the group as well otherwise). Review 5: a pod spec in a kind with no reader and a `kind` that is not a kind name are not audited through the ONE helper the subject reasons read (`notAudited` is non-empty whenever a reason says "not audited"); the verdict is one predicate, `Report.undetermined()`, read by `summary.score: null`/`summary.verdict`, the text headline, the gate's "incomplete" headline, `fix.after`, `ksi` (exit 1) and `fedramp_ksi`, and the posture report; an ExceptionList `notAudited:` entry accepts one object (`acceptedBy`, verdict determined); the row's `object` is cleaned once where it is built; an unknown kind in an unknown group is neither audited nor "not audited"; legacy binding names are refused by shape and `userNames: []` says why nobody is bound; `posture scan` reads OpenShift 3.x `v1` RBAC through the shared conversion; a non-string apiVersion gets one message; the SARIF note has a location and a fingerprint. Subject labels are sorted, so the bytes do not depend on `PYTHONHASHSEED`; a rule that declined on one object and fired on another is evaluated with the decline as a note, never in both `firedRules` and `notEvaluated`; every `cannot_decide` carries the rule's own reason, a platform gate says "does not apply on <platform>", and the three buckets partition the rule set. KN-N001 still fires on a whole namespace whose workloads no NetworkPolicy selects. The reasons are in every embedding of the record, with the counts and the names agreeing per control: `controlSummary.rulesNotEvaluated` and per-control `notEvaluatedRules` in `audit` (JSON, YAML, text grouped by reason with the JSON's count, and one SARIF `toolExecutionNotification` per reason), `audit_manifests` (with and without an exceptions file, compact or not), `fedramp_ksi` and `ksi` (JSON, text and the HTML page, which says "Not evaluated" with the reasons), the gate (JSON, Markdown summary and HTML coverage line), the OSCAL selection remarks, a posture snapshot's `coverage.notEvaluated` (naming the resource when the cluster refused it) and the posture report's standing in JSON, Markdown and HTML. A record whose caller kept no reasons still NAMES the rules that did not run, each with the record's own "no reason" phrase rather than one invented for it; a diff against a 0.9.2 snapshot reports nothing regressed, the bytes do not depend on document order, no input-derived text reaches a reason, and the exit code is unchanged. | G280 | **A check before an action that an agent may skip, or that reads "could not tell" as "fine", is not a check.** `kube-noob verify` answers allow, deny or not-determined for a change given as manifests or as the command line an agent intends to run, and an unread input is never an allow: a URL, `-f -` with no input, `exec`, a delete of anything but pods by name, a strategic-merge patch, `--context`, `--kuberc`, `KUBECONFIG` or `KUBECTL_EXTERNAL_DIFF` on the line, a flag or subcommand it does not know, a value left over after a flag kubectl reads only with `=` (`--validate`, `--dry-run`, `--cascade`), a file outside the tree or behind a link, a file the audit skipped, a file an earlier command on the same line may have written, a pod spec in a kind no rule reads, a deployer it does not model (`helmfile`, `argocd`, `flux`, `terraform` ...), a `kustomize --enable-*` build, and a line its shell reader cannot follow (a command word built at run time, a substitution in `${...}`, `$((...))` or an unquoted here-document, code piped into a shell, a kube tool inside `python -c`, `ssh`, `awk`, `git -c`, `vim -c` or `env -S`) are all not determined, with the reason in text, JSON, YAML, the `verify_change` tool and the hook message alike. The reader sees through the wrappers agents use (`sh -c`, `eval` of a literal, `env`, `sudo`, `timeout`, `xargs`, `find -exec`, quoting, `$'...'`, case and version variants of the tool's name, a full path, the `k` alias, `cd`, Makefile and package.json recipes, a script in the tree), never runs the command and never reads outside the working directory. `-f DIR` is read the way kubectl reads it (dotfiles and `.json` included, exact extensions, subdirectories only with `-R`). A dry run is the last `--dry-run` before `--`, and `none`/`false` is not one, in `diagnose` too. A day-2 change that cannot add a manifest finding (a restart, a scale, a label without a security key, deleting pods by name, a patch by name touching only safe fields) is allowed; a literal credential in `set env` is denied. The manifests are judged by `gate.run`, so verify allows exactly when the gate passes and denies whenever it fails on findings, on every rule fixture; exit codes add no meaning (0 allow, 1 deny, 5 not determined unless `--allow-undetermined`; a deny is never relaxed). `helm` is run only from an absolute PATH entry outside the tree, never on a chart with a link in it, with its output and its own words bounded and framed, in the gate too. `kube-noob hook claude-code` blocks with exit 2 on deny and on its own errors, asks the user (PreToolUse `ask`) on not-determined, answers inside its own deadline whatever the input, frames repository text as data and flags what reads as an instruction, asks for a PowerShell command that names a kube tool, never prints a JSON allow, and is turned off only from Claude Code's own environment. `hook install --write` merges atomically, refusing links, read-only files, repeated keys and a different kube-noob entry; the plugin's copy is off until the user opts in, is a `sh` guard that starts no program while off, and while on blocks by itself (exit 2) when uv is missing or fails, so it needs no `onFailure` and uv's absence or start-up reaches only users who turned it on; the `hook install` entry keeps `onFailure: block`. Second review: the reader's default is opaque -- a program it does not model is checked for a kube command in its arguments and for text naming a kube tool on its standard input, whatever it is called, so the next unlisted wrapper is not an allow, while one that names no kube command still passes; make is read through -f, -C, includes, $(shell), inline and continued recipes and command-line variables, npm-family scripts in the package their options name, sudo and timeout options by arity, and `function f {}`; cloud-identity annotations, --external-ip, `oc expose svc`, --from-file outside the tree and `helm plugin install` ask, and a URL with a password in `set env` denies. The deadline is a child process killed at the budget, the lexer's patterns are linear and a Makefile line over 4,096 characters is not read, and the plugin guard kills a uv run at 45 s. Every input-derived token in a message is quoted between « ». Third review: make reads every reachable target up to 5,000 and says when it stops; sudo -s/-i run a shell command line; just, go-task and deno tasks are read, other project runners and code that starts programs ask; xargs and parallel are read with their input; kubectl's subcommand is found the way cobra finds it, and a dynamic subcommand or an unknown root flag asks; one escalation-key table serves label, annotate and patch; cluster lifecycle tools ask; the hook's parent accepts only an `ask` on stdout. Fourth review: the allow set is the shipped contract and is written down in full -- a table in docs/use-cases.md of every shape that allows, asks or denies, each row run by a test, so the docs and the code cannot drift; the review's 223 ordinary commands and its documented asks are pinned as corpora. `port-forward` and `proxy` are their own class (`connects`), allowed unless the port is reachable from other hosts; a dry run on any verb that defines the flag persists nothing and is allowed, with a `note` when a real run of the manifest would be denied, and the flag earns nothing on `exec` or a plugin; a command that carries a manifest with an explicit target (`--context`, `--cluster`, `--kubeconfig`, `KUBECONFIG=`) is not determined because the platform the manifest is judged against is that context's, while a day-2 command with no manifest is judged the same with or without one; `--help` on any verb, `oc adm node-logs`, `must-gather`, `release info`, the `upgrade` status, `oc registry info`, `krew list|info|search`, `neat` and `helm diff` are allowed, and attach, extract, login, logout, registry login, krew install and `config use-context` ask with their own reason; a command past the reader's 20,000-character cap is allowed when it names no kube tool anywhere and not determined when it does; verify and the hook write under an ASCII encoding instead of exiting 4; the hook's timeout message states the budget it ran with. Fifth review: `--dry-run` is read with pflag's arity rule (a bare non-bool flag takes the next word, an unknown bare flag fails closed), for helm too, and a run-time word after it asks; past the 20,000-character cap only one inert echo, printf or here-document is allowed; `delete --raw`, `-f` or a second kind is not a pod by name, nor is any day-2 change with a run-time word, and the last `-p` patch is the one judged; `--help` is allowed on built-in verbs only; `--post-renderer` and kustomize's `--enable-*` ask on every verb first; `--as`, `--token`, `--server`, `--user` and the certificate flags ask on every change; every credential form is redacted from the printed reason; `oc adm` allows only its read list; PowerShell constructs that build or run a command ask; `set env` of PATH, proxy and CA variables, `expose --type` that is not a literal ClusterIP, control-plane namespaces and workloads, `create secret docker-registry`, `neat` with a non-read verb, a widened proxy filter and `must-gather --image` ask; hidden characters are marked `<U+XXXX>` identically under every encoding; the review's 1,106 lines are pinned in three corpora. | | G293 | **A permission matrix written by hand drifts the day a reader adds a resource; one recorded from the reads the code makes cannot.** `docs/permissions.md` is generated from each live capability run against a recording backend; the chart's Role and ClusterRole grant exactly the allowlist (namespaced part, for the Role) plus `pods/log`, verbs `get` and `list`, never Secrets; a forbidden list is reported as not read, naming the resource. | `tests/test_permissions_doc.py::test_the_document_is_current_and_the_check_refuses_a_stale_one`, `tests/test_permissions_doc.py::test_every_live_capability_has_a_row_and_every_row_is_recorded_not_written`, `tests/test_permissions_doc.py::test_the_chart_grants_exactly_what_the_code_can_read`, `tests/test_permissions_doc.py::test_a_capability_that_reads_only_a_few_resources_says_so`, `tests/test_permissions_doc.py::test_a_forbidden_list_is_reported_as_not_read_naming_the_resource_never_as_none`, `tests/test_permissions_doc.py::test_every_read_of_every_row_names_itself_when_forbidden`, `tests/test_permissions_doc.py::test_the_watch_and_the_debugger_name_their_bonus_reads_when_forbidden`, `tests/test_permissions_doc.py::test_kubectl_s_newer_sentence_for_an_api_that_is_not_served_is_recognised`, `tests/test_permissions_doc.py::test_an_absent_api_a_gone_pod_and_a_missing_serviceaccount_are_not_read_failures`, `ci:Permission matrix matches what the live capabilities read and what the chart grants` |
| G294 | **"Works offline" is a transcript or it is a claim.** `scripts/airgap_check.sh` installs the built wheel from a local directory inside `unshare -rn`, proves the namespace on itself first, runs doctor, an audit, a must-gather analysis and one MCP request with each exit code asserted, and refuses with exit 2 where it cannot create the namespace; CI runs it on the wheel the publishable job built, and the install guide states what it does and does not establish. | `tests/test_airgap_check.py::test_the_script_is_executable_and_parses`, `tests/test_airgap_check.py::test_no_wheel_is_a_refusal_that_names_what_is_missing_never_a_pass`, `tests/test_airgap_check.py::test_an_unknown_subcommand_or_a_missing_executable_is_exit_2`, `tests/test_airgap_check.py::test_the_network_proof_and_the_four_checks_run_in_a_namespace_and_each_exit_is_asserted`, `tests/test_airgap_check.py::test_two_wheels_in_the_directory_is_a_refusal_naming_both`, `tests/test_airgap_check.py::test_no_unshare_with_passwordless_sudo_is_exit_2_with_the_sentence_never_127`, `tests/test_airgap_check.py::test_a_check_that_exits_with_the_wrong_code_fails_the_transcript`, `tests/test_airgap_check.py::test_ci_runs_the_transcript_on_the_built_wheel_and_install_md_says_what_it_proves`, `ci:airgap` |
| G296 | **A review finding that is not written down is found again.** About three hundred confirmed defects across eleven branches fell into twenty-six classes that recur because they are about how an author tests what they built and not what they skipped. `docs/review-classes.md` keeps the classes, each with the shape it took and the test shape that holds it, and AGENTS.md sends every author to it before pushing; the table is gapless, numbered for good, and its count in AGENTS.md is held by a test | `tests/test_review_classes.py::test_the_classes_are_numbered_without_a_gap`, `tests/test_review_classes.py::test_agents_md_states_the_number_of_classes_the_table_has`, `tests/test_review_classes.py::test_every_class_names_a_test_shape` |
| G297 | **The two documents every change appends to are merged by union, so no merge stops on a line nobody disagrees about -- and nothing whose lines are rewritten joins them.** `CHANGELOG.md` (a bullet prepended under `## Unreleased`) and `docs/guidance.md` (a `G<n>` row appended at the end) are append-only by construction, so two branches cut from one `main` always collide there and nowhere else; `merge=union` keeps both sides' lines. A generated document keeps the ordinary merge: union would keep both versions of a rewritten number. The union list is exhaustive and tested as such, and the merged result is still checked by `scripts/doc_numbers.py --check` and the duplicate-id and missing-citation checks in `tests/test_guidance.py` | `tests/test_merge_attributes.py::test_the_append_only_documents_merge_by_union`, `tests/test_merge_attributes.py::test_no_generated_document_merges_by_union`, `tests/test_merge_attributes.py::test_union_is_declared_for_nothing_else`, `tests/test_merge_attributes.py::test_two_bullets_prepended_to_the_unreleased_section_both_survive`, `tests/test_merge_attributes.py::test_two_rows_appended_to_guidance_both_survive`, `tests/test_merge_attributes.py::test_a_generated_number_both_sides_rewrote_still_conflicts`, `tests/test_guidance.py::test_every_rule_has_existing_checks` |
| G310 | **A deterministic verdict nobody can re-derive is a claim, not evidence -- and a verifier that cannot get an input must never report a pass.** `audit --receipt PATH` and `gate --receipt PATH` write one self-describing document binding the schema, the tool version, every input by SHA-256 with the path as given and the path it resolved to, one digest over the whole input set, a **meaning version per rule** derived from the rule's own declaration rather than hand-maintained, the settings that changed the verdict, the scope read and not read, and the verdict (counts, the score and the reason there is no verdict **as `Report.undetermined()` decided them** -- a receipt records the verdict it was given and never re-judges it -- the exit code, which is 1 for an undetermined report whatever `--fail-on` says, and every finding by `Finding.fingerprint()`). `receipt verify` answers identical (0), differs and names the fields (1), or CANNOT BE CHECKED (5) -- for a missing input, a digest that moved, another kube-noob's version, a rule set that no longer means the same thing, a file added to a directory since, standard input, or a run that cannot be replayed from the files the receipt binds. A changed input is a different question, never a changed verdict; a payload that does not match its own digest is refused (4), not checked. The verdict payload is byte-identical for identical inputs and everything that cannot be deterministic lives in a separate envelope, which records no host and no runner identity and says why. SHA-256 only: the document states in its own text that it proves the same inputs give the same verdict and proves nothing about who produced it | | G298 | **A generated document that only the release checks is a document the release finds stale.** `docs/sbom.spdx.json` and the krew manifest are generated, and their `--check` form ran in `release.yml` only, so a pull request could leave either behind and the tag paid for it. Both run in `ci/lint` and in `check.sh` now | `tests/test_check_sh.py::test_every_gate_a_ci_code_job_runs_is_run_by_check_sh`, `tests/test_sbom.py::test_the_committed_document_is_current`, `tests/test_krew.py::test_the_committed_script_and_manifest_are_current` |
| G320 | **A failure that cannot name its own cause costs more than the failure.** The e2e install handler dumped events only, so a crash-looping init container reported that it was backing off and never what it printed; the `e2e-kind (OpenShift 4.22, Kubernetes 1.35)` leg failed this way on main and on three branches with nothing in the log to act on, while 4.20, 4.21 and Kubernetes 1.37 passed. Events describe the kubelet's view, logs describe the program's. `scripts/ci_pod_failure.sh` now prints both, for every container of every pod, init containers first and `--previous` beside the current attempt, and both call sites use the one script rather than two copies of a one-line handler. `ci:e2e-kind` and `ci:policy-kind` call `scripts/ci_pod_failure.sh` on an install that does not become ready. |
| G319 | **A permission tightened on files silently tightened the directory holding them.** `Containerfile.db`'s `COPY --chmod=0444` into `/kube-noob-db/` left that directory at `0444` under a newer BuildKit, which creates the destination for a multi-source copy. Without the execute bit nothing non-root could traverse it, so the archive was unreadable to the image's own user (1001) and to the chart's init container, and the only visible symptom was `cp: cannot stat ...: Permission denied` -- a message about the file, caused by the directory. Root was unaffected, which is why it survived every local build. The directory is created and chmodded in its own layer before the files arrive, and the `db-image` job now reads the archive as 1001 immediately after the build so the failure names its own cause. Verified by re-running the job unchanged against `8867b7c`, where it had passed, and watching it fail the same way: the cause is the builder on the runner, not the tree. `ci:db-image` reads `/kube-noob-db/ATTRIBUTION.md` as `--user 1001:0`. |
| G321 | **A version gate a model evaluates must not be a number comparison.** The seven directory skills told Claude to refuse an engine "lower than 0.9.3" and to say "nothing has been audited". `0.10.0` is newer by PEP 440 and lower as text, so the next release turns that instruction into a refusal of a correct engine, in all seven skills at once, with a message asserting no audit happened -- the product's own rule broken in the plugin that advertises it. The gate is now the `forModel` key, which observes what the engine can do rather than parsing its version, and each skill says not to compare version numbers. `tests/test_directory_plugin.py::test_an_older_engine_is_named_as_older_and_not_read` now refuses the phrase "lower than" in the block and requires the instruction not to compare. |
| G322 | **Fixing one instance of a defect is not fixing the defect.** G319 repaired `Containerfile.db`'s `COPY --chmod=0444` into a directory the builder creates, and did not look for the same shape elsewhere. `Containerfile.seed` had it, so `/seed` was mode `0444`, the init container at uid 1001 could not traverse it, and `e2e-kind (OpenShift 4.22, Kubernetes 1.35)` stayed red on main and every branch for three days with `cp: cannot stat '/seed/jira-snapshot.tgz.enc': Permission denied` -- the same sentence as G319's, about a different file. The documented seed-image snippet in `docs/use-cases.md` handed readers the identical defect. The guard is now structural rather than per-file: a `COPY --chmod` whose mode has no execute bit must be preceded by a `RUN mkdir` that makes its destination traversable. `tests/test_image_copy_modes.py::test_every_chmod_copy_has_a_traversable_destination_directory` and `::test_the_documented_snippet_does_not_hand_a_user_the_same_defect`, each seen red against each Containerfile with its fix removed. |
| G328 | **A caveat a consumer does not read is a caveat that is not there; the honest half of the answer has to travel in the array every reader parses.** `audit --format sarif` on one privileged Deployment emitted twelve results, all of them findings, and put all thirty-six statements about what it had NOT evaluated -- including `not evaluated, no Service in the input: KN-N004, ...` over fifty-five rules -- into `invocations[0].toolExecutionNotifications`. That is where SARIF puts what a tool says about its own run, and it is not what the consumers this file exists for parse: GitHub code scanning raises alerts from `runs[].results`, and DefectDojo and the scanner operators import findings from `runs[].results`. So the file arrived at the other end as twelve findings and nothing else, and a repository reading `12 alerts` had no way to know that fifty-five rules reached no verdict -- an absence reading as a complete answer, one layer out from the verdict, in the format this product leads with. The remedy already existed twice in the same file and had not been carried across: `sarif_of_gate` emits the gate's incomplete state "as a property of the invocation AND one note-level result, so a consumer that reads only results sees it" (G272), and `_not_audited_results` emits one `kind: review` result per object no rule read, "so code scanning lists it in the file view and tracks it". The unevaluated RULES were the neighbour nobody fixed -- `docs/review-classes.md` class 21, and class 6 (the caveat exists only in JSON) with the JSON one hop further away. The unevaluated rules are now ONE result, not one per reason: a Deployment-only input leaves fifty-five rules unevaluated under thirty-three reasons, and thirty-three near-identical alerts are what gets a rule muted in code scanning, which would switch the signal off more completely than the notification did -- the claim is about the run as a whole, so one alert carries every reason and its fingerprint covers the set, so it changes when coverage changes and closes when nothing is left. A file that was not read is the opposite shape and stays one alert per file with its own location, because that one IS about a place and is fixed file by file. The notifications stay as well: a reader of the invocation keeps what it had, and saying it twice is the better failure of the two. Both new rules say in words how to close the gap and that it is not a pass, because a note-level alert with no remedy is one a reviewer dismisses. Two `kind` conventions now sit in this file -- the gate's undetermined result omits `kind`, so SARIF reads it as `fail`, while these and `notAudited` use `kind: review` -- and that is left alone rather than quietly changed, because lowering an existing signal's severity deserves its own decision (`docs/open-questions.md`) | `tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_the_rules_that_reached_no_verdict_are_a_result_and_not_only_a_notification`, `tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_the_count_in_the_result_is_the_count_in_the_json`, `tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_every_unevaluated_rule_id_is_named_in_the_result`, `tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_an_input_that_leaves_nothing_unevaluated_raises_no_coverage_alert`, `tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_a_file_that_was_not_read_is_one_result_per_file_with_its_own_location`, `tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_the_notifications_are_still_there_so_neither_kind_of_consumer_loses_it`, `tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_the_document_is_valid_sarif`, `tests/test_sarif_coverage_results.py::test_the_coverage_rules_say_what_to_do_and_never_that_it_passed` |
| G327 | **A tool a profile withheld must read as withheld, never as a tool kube-noob does not have.** Fifty-six tools is more than an MCP client lists usefully and more than a model chooses well between, so `mcp --tools incident|review|compliance|learn` offers fifteen to twenty-three of them. The hazard is not the smaller list; it is what a model concludes from an absence. A client offered the `incident` profile cannot see `kubenoob_fedramp_ksi`, and a model that is not told why does not answer "this cannot be checked here" -- it answers the compliance question from its own knowledge, and the user reads an unaudited answer in a session that looked audited. That is this product's own defect, an absence reading as a complete answer, moved from a verdict into the tool surface. So every withheld tool is named in the server's instructions with the flag that offers it, and the instructions say not to answer from knowledge and not to say kube-noob cannot do it. The hand-written instructions are the second source of truth here (class 9): they recommend tools by name, a profile can withhold one of them, and a test holds that every `kubenoob_*` name the prose mentions is either registered or listed as withheld -- never recommended and silently missing. An unknown `--tools` value exits 2 rather than falling back to all or to none, because a server offering some other set of tools than the one asked for says so nowhere in the session. A profile decides only what is LISTED: `select` hands back the same `ToolSpec` objects, so there is no per-profile variant of a tool to drift, and two servers started differently answer the same call identically. The two reasons a tool is missing stay distinct because they have different fixes -- a profile withheld it (start with another `--tools`) or a capability tier is off (a decision about what kube-noob may do, which no profile overrides) -- and the tier filter in `Toolkit.specs` runs first, so a tier-gated tool can never be reported as one a profile hid. `config_status` is in every profile for that reason. Found while writing it: `learn` named `tutor`, which is an MCP prompt and not a tool, so the profile would have offered ten tools while its author believed it offered eleven -- which is why the typo test reads the names from the source rather than from one configuration, since no single `load_settings` produces the whole surface | `tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_a_withheld_tool_is_named_in_the_instructions`, `tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_the_prose_never_recommends_a_tool_the_server_does_not_list`, `tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_an_unknown_profile_is_a_usage_error_not_a_quiet_fallback`, `tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_the_cli_exits_2_on_an_unknown_profile`, `tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_no_profile_names_a_tool_that_does_not_exist`, `tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_every_tool_is_reachable_through_some_profile`, `tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_a_profile_changes_what_is_listed_and_never_what_a_tool_answers`, `tests/test_tool_profiles.py::test_the_core_tools_are_in_every_profile` |
| G326 | **A test that measures the runner reports a false failure, which is the mirror image of the false pass this product exists to prevent.** G263 replaced two wall-clock budgets with assertions about the code and left thirty-four of the thirty-six in the tree, thirty-one of them below thirty seconds; two of those have since blocked merges. `test_hostile_and_oversized_input_is_bounded_and_fast` asserted `< 1.0` beside an exact cap assertion and measured 1.0239 s and 1.0607 s on PR #77's `test (3.11)` job, after the identical sha had gone 19 of 19 green on a `workflow_dispatch` run; the work it timed is 0.001 s, so the budget had a thousandfold headroom and still lost -- it measured a second of descheduling, not a second of work, and the cap beside it was the whole contract, so the clock is gone. `test_a_post_that_cannot_get_a_slot_is_refused_with_503_within_the_wait` asserted `waited < 3` with the server's own `SLOT_WAIT_SECONDS` patched to 0.3 s: ten times the deadline it claimed to be about, unable to tell an honoured deadline from an ignored one, and it failed at 3.1 s. It now derives its ceiling from that constant, so an ignored deadline (5.0 s shipped) fails and a stall does not. Two more were found by the guard rather than by grep, and are the tightest in the suite: `test_no_crafted_line_costs_more_than_a_few_milliseconds` asserted a PER-CALL average of 15 ms against a measured 1.2-4.2 ms, under four times, and dividing by the loop count divides out the loop but not a stall. `tests/bounds.py` is the one helper: a call site states the SHAPE of the regression it guards -- linear, cubic risk, backtracking, a small multiple, fixed work, no hang -- and the shape names the smallest regression as a multiple of the measured honest time; the ceiling is the geometric mean of the two, so the assertion sits as far above the noise as below the regression. The environment enters twice, because the two things that go wrong on a shared runner differ in kind: multiplicatively for a coverage tracer (measured 1.0x on the regex-dominated ceph scan and 1.51x on the Python-heavy SARIF merge here) and additively for a stall, which no multiplier models. Both are frozen at import, so no fixture or test order can move a later ceiling. Every failure prints the elapsed time, the ceiling, its derivation and the regression it is for, because `assert 1.06 < 1.0` is why these were dismissed as flakes rather than read. A ceiling that could not catch the regression its shape names raises `VacuousBound` instead of passing, which is what refused a clock for the ceph site. Three contrast tests, where the signal is the gap between a fast call answered and a fast call queued behind a slow one, could not be made loose: two now assert the ORDER of completion, which no stall can move, and the third states the gap it needs and refuses the call site when the slow side is too small. One site could not be made both meaningful and stable by any ceiling and became a ratio instead, which is G263's own fix: `test_diagnose_lists_its_signals_and_stays_fast_on_hostile_text` honestly costs 5.0 s, so the linear shape asks for 104 s and `MAX_CEILING_SECONDS` caps it at the 30 s it already had -- under six times the honest cost, and it measured 32.5 s, 35.7 s and 37.1 s in three of twenty-five runs under load. It now diagnoses half and full of each paste back to back and holds the ratio under 3.0, measured at 1.40 to 1.73 where quadratic is about 4.0. Of the thirty-six, thirty-one went to the helper, one clock was deleted outright, three became ratio or ordering assertions, and one documented sixty-second ceiling was left alone. What none of this catches, and is not asked to: a wrong answer produced quickly, and work that got faster by being skipped -- so every call site keeps its deterministic assertions, and several kept only them. `tests/test_timing_assertions.py` parses the test tree and refuses a new raw wall-clock comparison below the helper's own loosest ceiling, with no allowlist of the existing sites (class 9); `process_time`, a ratio between two measurements and a stated catastrophe ceiling are exempt structurally. This is an instance of `docs/review-classes.md` class 10, recurring as class 21 | `tests/test_timing_assertions.py::test_no_test_asserts_a_tight_wall_clock_budget`, `tests/test_timing_assertions.py::test_the_guard_sees_every_shape_of_tight_assertion`, `tests/test_timing_assertions.py::test_the_guard_lets_through_what_is_not_the_defect`, `tests/test_ceph_health.py::test_hostile_and_oversized_input_is_bounded_and_fast`, `tests/test_alert_report_review2.py::test_a_post_that_cannot_get_a_slot_is_refused_with_503_within_the_wait`, `tests/test_capability_routes.py::test_no_crafted_line_costs_more_than_a_few_milliseconds`, `tests/test_alert_report_review3.py::test_mcp_reports_waiting_for_a_slot_do_not_starve_other_tools`, `tests/test_admission.py::test_a_padded_object_does_not_outrun_the_webhooks_timeout` |
| G325 | **A typo in a key is an unrecognised shape, not an absence, and must not share the absence's answer.** `apiversion: v1` beside a correct `kind: Pod` was classified by `gate._classify` exactly as a GitHub Actions workflow in the same directory is -- "not a Kubernetes manifest (no document with apiVersion and kind)" -- so a **privileged** pod was listed under `skipped`, `passed` stayed `true`, `incomplete` stayed `false`, `gate -f DIR` exited 0, and `verify -f DIR` answered `allow` before the apply. Kubernetes keys are case-sensitive, so the API server would reject that document too; the honest answer is that the file was not read as a manifest, which makes the gate incomplete. A required key that is absent exactly and present as a spelling differing only by letter case, surrounding whitespace or `-`/`_` separators is now named as the typo it is, and that one reason -- marked `forbidsVerdict` on the entry `skipped` already carried, not in a second list -- makes `passed` false, `incomplete` true, and the exit code 5, beside `unreadable`'s and for G126's reason: a file the walk declined to read is not a policy violation (1). In `verify -f` it appends to `undetermined`, so the verdict is `not-determined` (exit 5) through the mechanism the ~40 unmodellable command shapes already use. The document is NOT normalised and audited: auditing input the API server would reject as if it were valid is the same false answer pointing the other way. Deliberately narrow -- a kustomization, a Helm values file or a CRD fragment with a `kind` and no `apiVersion` at all is not a near miss -- because widening it would turn every repository scanned with `gate -f .` red. `tests/test_near_miss_manifest.py::test_the_gate_exits_five_and_not_one_and_not_zero`, `::test_verify_on_a_directory_holding_a_near_miss_is_not_determined`, `::test_a_repository_of_non_manifests_beside_one_good_manifest_still_exits_zero` (the negative half) and `::test_every_skip_reason_decides_whether_it_forbids_a_verdict`, which fails when a reason reaches `_classify` with no decision beside it in `gate.SKIP_REASONS`, and `::test_a_file_nested_past_the_loader_limit_forbids_a_verdict_too` for the one other reason that decision changed. |
| G323 | **A compliance report is only as complete as the rules nobody removed.** The OpenShift Compliance Operator's own output is where "compliant" is easiest to get wrong, because four different absences all look like a pass: a rule a `TailoredProfile` disabled, a `ComplianceCheckResult` of `NOT-APPLICABLE`, `INFO`, `MANUAL`, `ERROR` or `INCONSISTENT`, a rule the profile lists that no result names at all, and a `PASS` from a `ComplianceScan` that never reached `DONE` or reported an error. `compliance results` therefore answers with the rules the profile SAID would be evaluated, each `pass`, `fail` or `not-evaluated` with the object that proves it, and a `ComplianceSuite` reporting `COMPLIANT` over any of them is `undetermined` -- never compliant -- with the suite's claim printed beside the reason it does not hold. One predicate (`undeterminedBecause`) decides the headline for the text view, the JSON, the YAML, the MCP result and `--require-complete` alike, and the pass rate is null with the reason beside it while that list is non-empty, the shape `audit.model.Report.undetermined` already uses for the score. Two corollaries that are not symmetrical: an absence never makes a pass, and it never withdraws a `FAIL` the operator did report; and an input with no Compliance Operator objects is undetermined, never "the operator is not installed", which is why this reads files and must-gathers and asks no cluster for anything | | G330 | **A tool's answer is read by a model, so the caveat has to be where a model will still see it.** Three MCP tools returned an answer in which nothing said the audit had not read the object. `posture_scan` knew two families of wrong apiVersion -- routing kinds under another group, RBAC kinds under a legacy one -- and a workload kind fell past both: a privileged `Deployment` under `apps.k8s.io/v1`, which the API server would never serve and `refusal` refuses outright, was ranked as a real `apps/v1` Deployment and reported as `workloads: 1`, `0` attack paths, `notRead: []`. `threat_model` is built on the same `Inventory` and said the same by saying nothing. And `fedramp_ksi` set `verdict: undetermined` after the indicator crosswalk, so a dict in insertion order put it at byte 54,945 of 55,269 and an MCP client that truncates a long result dropped exactly the word that withholds the page's claim. `Inventory.add` now asks the audit's own `refusal` -- one detector, not a third local copy of what a wrong group looks like -- before ranking any kind it models, and records a refusal under the gated `refused` cause; the KSI verdict is inserted first, ahead of the bulk, so a 1 KiB prefix still carries it. The rule for the next tool: an output field that only a complete reader sees is review class 6 again, and the place to put the limit is the front | `tests/test_mcp_verdict_reaches_the_client.py::test_posture_scan_does_not_rank_an_object_the_audit_refused`, `tests/test_mcp_verdict_reaches_the_client.py::test_posture_scan_still_ranks_the_same_object_under_the_group_it_knows`, `tests/test_mcp_verdict_reaches_the_client.py::test_an_object_with_no_api_version_is_still_ranked`, `tests/test_mcp_verdict_reaches_the_client.py::test_threat_model_names_the_object_it_could_not_read`, `tests/test_mcp_verdict_reaches_the_client.py::test_the_ksi_verdict_survives_a_client_that_truncates`, `tests/test_mcp_verdict_reaches_the_client.py::test_a_determined_ksi_page_carries_no_verdict_word` |
| G331 | **The file a pipeline uploads is the one that decides what a reviewer sees.** G328 put the rules that reached no verdict into `runs[].results` for `audit --format sarif`, and `sarif_of_gate` -- the renderer behind `gate --sarif`, which is what `scripts/ci/gate.sh` hands to code scanning -- was not changed. So the uploaded file carried fourteen findings and nothing else for a report whose own `controlSummary` said fifty-two rules reached no verdict, and for an input made only of kinds no rule reads it carried **zero results**, which is how SARIF spells "nothing found". The lesson was recorded in the same module, one function along, and not carried there: review class 21 in the file that documents it. `sarif_of_gate` now builds the same `coverage` record, normalising the two keys that differ from the audit's report -- `unreadable` rows carry `file` here and `path` there, and the input uri comes from `files` because a gate finding has no `file` -- and a file the walk declined to read as a manifest (`forbidsVerdict`, G325) is the same absence, so it gets the same result rather than being left as the one file-level gap with no alert. G272's `kube-noob/undetermined` result stays: the two answer different questions. The rule for the next renderer: when a caveat is added to one of two renderers of the same report, the other one is the defect, and the uploaded artifact is the one that matters most | `tests/test_gate_sarif_coverage.py::test_the_uploaded_file_says_what_the_run_did_not_evaluate`, `tests/test_gate_sarif_coverage.py::test_an_input_of_kinds_no_rule_reads_is_not_an_empty_run`, `tests/test_gate_sarif_coverage.py::test_the_gate_and_the_audit_agree_on_the_coverage_they_report`, `tests/test_gate_sarif_coverage.py::test_a_file_the_walk_would_not_read_as_a_manifest_is_a_result`, `tests/test_gate_sarif_coverage.py::test_an_unreadable_file_keyed_on_file_rather_than_path_still_anchors`, `tests/test_gate_sarif_coverage.py::test_a_run_that_evaluated_everything_raises_no_coverage_alert`, `tests/test_gate_sarif_coverage.py::test_the_undetermined_result_is_still_there` |
| G329 | **A reader of the gate's report must read its verdict, not re-derive one.** G272 and G281 made `audit` and `gate` answer `undetermined` for an object the rules did not read and for a rule that declined on one. Three readers of that report kept an older idea of what incomplete means and turned it back into a clean answer one hop out. `verify` built its own list (`_unread_pods`: a pod spec in a kind with no reader, and nothing else), so a privileged `Deployment` under `apps.k8s.io/v1`, the same object as `kind: deployment`, a `ClusterRoleBinding` to `cluster-admin` under a group the rules refuse, and a control-plane rule that declined were each `allow`, exit 0 -- printed above the audit's own line saying "it was not audited, so this verdict is undetermined", and returned as `"verdict": "allow"` to a model through `verify_change`. `insight`, the tool the server's instructions tell a model to start with, carried the audit's findings and nothing else of it, so the same object came back as `ran: audit` beside an empty list. And the CI wrapper computed `evidence` from `notRun` alone, so with every optional check supplied it wrote `evidence=complete` and "merge blocked · evidence: complete" for a report whose own `incomplete` was `true`. `verify` now reads the gate's one predicate (`report["undetermined"]`) and answers `not-determined` (exit 5; a deny beside it stays 1, and an object an exception accepted under `notAudited` is the gate's answer here too); `insight` names the audit's reason under `skipped`; the wrapper puts the reason first in `because`, ahead of the standing entries so the 600-character cut cannot remove it, and reads `incomplete` in any shape. `_unread_pods` is kept, because `verify`'s collector reads what `kubectl apply` would send -- .json files and dot-directories the gate's collector skips -- so a pod spec in one of those reaches no gate report at all; but it now passes over an object an `ExceptionList` entry ACCEPTED, which is the one case where an object is unaudited and the gate's verdict still determined. It had never seen the exception list, so it refused the object anyway while the gate honoured the entry, and the remedy the tool documents did not work at the hop that runs before an action. An OPEN row is deliberately still named by both: the gate's line withholds the verdict and names the object, this one names the file and what is in it that no rule read, and passing over those too left the verdict right and the reason emptier. The rule for the next reader: a second list of what makes a report incomplete is out of date the day the first one learns a new reason (classes 9 and 21) | `tests/test_undetermined_hops.py::test_verify_never_allows_what_the_gate_calls_incomplete`, `tests/test_undetermined_hops.py::test_verify_still_allows_what_the_gate_passes_and_denies_what_it_fails`, `tests/test_undetermined_hops.py::test_the_verify_command_and_the_mcp_tool_say_not_determined`, `tests/test_undetermined_hops.py::test_insight_says_the_audit_reached_no_verdict`, `tests/test_undetermined_hops.py::test_an_undetermined_gate_is_never_beside_complete_evidence`, `tests/test_undetermined_hops.py::test_the_wrapper_reads_incomplete_in_any_shape`, `tests/test_undetermined_hops.py::test_a_gate_that_is_not_incomplete_keeps_complete_evidence`, `tests/test_undetermined_hops.py::test_the_real_gate_and_the_wrapper_agree_on_an_undetermined_audit`, `tests/test_undetermined_hops.py::test_an_exception_the_gate_accepts_is_accepted_by_verify_too`, `tests/test_undetermined_hops.py::test_an_open_row_keeps_the_detail_the_gate_line_does_not_carry` |
| G332 | **A verdict about a directory is a verdict about every file in it, including the ones the walk did not open.** `gate -f k8s/` kept `.yaml` and `.yml` and did not enter dot-directories, and a file outside that was recorded nowhere -- not in `files`, not in `skipped`, not in `unreadable`. So a directory holding `pod.json` (privileged, `hostPID`) and `.hidden/pod.yaml` (privileged, `hostNetwork`) answered exit 0, `passed: true`, `skipped: []`, while `audit -f k8s/` on the same directory reported eleven findings. `skipped: []` is not a thin answer, it is a false one: the gate asserted it had passed over nothing. The walk now records any file it declined to open that **demonstrably holds a manifest** -- `_holds_a_manifest` opens it and looks for a document carrying both `apiVersion` and `kind` -- under the `NOT_OPENED_PREFIX` skip reason, which forbids the verdict, and names the rule that excluded it plus the remedy. Deciding from the extension instead is what the first version did, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is a `.yml` file in a dot-directory in every repository, so it held every real gate run incomplete: an alert on every repository is one nobody reads, which is a worse outcome than the gap it replaces, and `test_directory_walks_skip_what_is_not_a_manifest` caught it. A file that does not parse is not called a manifest either -- `kubectl apply -f` would not send it and the walk never claimed to read it. Reading a file to decide does not audit it and does not change which files are audited. Which extensions the walk SHOULD read stays open -- `audit` enters dot-directories and the gate does not, and `verify` reads `.json` -- and is a product decision; this is only the part that is true under every answer to it. `tests/test_verify.py::test_verify_f_reads_a_directory_the_way_kubectl_does` had asserted the gate PASSED such a directory, and the same test asserts `verify` and the kubectl-command path both deny because of the privileged pod in it -- three commands, one directory, and the one people put in CI was the one that said clean. That assertion was the defect and is now inverted; the collector is unchanged and still audits only the file it was ever going to audit. The first version reported a file by NAME, which broke every ConfigMap volume mount: such a mount exposes each key twice -- `key` is a symlink to `..data/key`, and `..data` is a symlink to a timestamped directory -- so the walk read the first and reported the second as an unopened manifest, taking the Tekton Task in `gate-ci` from `passed` to `evidence-incomplete`, exit 5, for anyone passing manifests into a Pod the standard way. `..data` is the exact case the walk's own comment names as its reason for skipping dot-directories. A candidate is now compared by `(st_dev, st_ino)` against the files actually opened, in two passes because `sorted` reaches `..data/` before `gate.yaml`. The rule for the next walk: a filter that drops an input is a statement about that input, the place to record it is the report rather than the filter, and whether two paths are the same input is decided by identity and never by name | `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_a_directory_with_an_unopened_manifest_is_not_a_pass`, `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_the_reason_says_which_rule_excluded_it_and_what_to_do`, `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_the_exit_code_is_evidence_incomplete_not_findings`, `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_a_file_that_could_not_hold_a_manifest_is_passed_over_silently`, `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_a_directory_of_only_opened_manifests_still_passes`, `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_the_same_directory_without_the_unopened_files_exits_zero`, `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_a_workflow_file_does_not_hold_the_gate_incomplete`, `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_a_file_that_does_not_parse_is_not_called_a_manifest`, `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_a_json_file_that_is_not_a_manifest_is_passed_over`, `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_a_configmap_volume_mount_is_not_two_files`, `tests/test_gate_walk_candidates.py::test_a_dot_directory_copy_that_is_a_DIFFERENT_file_is_still_a_gap` |
| G339 | **`ready` is a verdict, so the tool that says it reads the audit's verdict.** `run_protocol` -- the MCP tool whose own description says "use this first for any design/review request", and `kube-noob protocol` behind it -- decided `ready` from an absence of HIGH findings and never read `Report.undetermined()`, the one predicate every other renderer of that report reads. So one privileged `docker:24-dind` container was `blocked` in a `Deployment` and `ready` in an Argo `Rollout`: exit 0, `remainingBlockers: []`, `exposedScenarios: []`, and `kubectl apply -f manifest.yaml` printed underneath, while `auditValidationBrief.after.verdict` in the same result said `undetermined`. The comment above the decision had named the gap ("until the coverage record lands") and the record landed without this reader. The status is `undetermined` now whenever the audit's is, with `statusReason` beside it and ahead of the long fields; a blocker stays `blocked` and carries the reason too, because a finding and an unread object are independent facts; the scenarios the red team could not decide are listed as `undecidedScenarios` beside the exposed ones instead of only inside `steps`. The command exits 5 on it, and 4 on a status its table does not know -- the table's default was 0, so the one status that withholds the verdict exited the way `ready` does. Negative control first: a generated workload and the generator's own output handed back for review are still `ready`, exit 0. | `tests/test_protocol_undetermined.py::test_an_object_the_audit_did_not_read_is_not_ready`, `tests/test_protocol_undetermined.py::test_a_generated_workload_is_still_ready`, `tests/test_protocol_undetermined.py::test_a_reviewed_manifest_the_audit_read_in_full_is_still_ready`, `tests/test_protocol_undetermined.py::test_the_command_does_not_exit_0_on_an_undetermined_run`, `tests/test_protocol_undetermined.py::test_a_status_the_command_does_not_know_is_not_a_pass`, `tests/test_protocol_undetermined.py::test_a_blocker_beside_an_unread_object_is_blocked_and_says_what_was_not_read`, `tests/test_protocol_undetermined.py::test_the_scenarios_that_could_not_be_decided_are_beside_the_ones_that_were` |
| G336 | **A section of this repository's docs is published verbatim by the site repository, and nothing here said so.** The site's `generate.py` has `roadmap_status()`, which lifts the `## Status` section of `docs/roadmap.md` into `/insights/`, and the site then refuses three plugin-index strings on every page it writes itself, because that pull request is not open and the command does not work. A row naming the command was moved INTO `## Status` here, which turned the site's build red -- found a week later by a session sourced at the other repository, with the product commit long since merged, and findable by nothing in this repository. The row now says what is waiting without naming the command, and `tests/test_published_roadmap_status.py` holds those strings out of that section, extracting it with the site's own expression. That is deliberately a second copy of another repository's assertion, which is normally review class 9: the reason it is right here is that the two cannot be merged -- the string is authored in this repository and rendered in that one, so a check in only one place is a check that fires after the fact. The rest of the roadmap may still discuss the work, and a test holds that too, because emptying a roadmap to satisfy a website is the tail wagging the dog. The rule for the next cross-repository contract: whatever another repository publishes from this one needs its guard HERE, where the content is written | `tests/test_published_roadmap_status.py::test_the_published_status_section_offers_no_install_the_site_refuses`, `tests/test_published_roadmap_status.py::test_the_rest_of_the_roadmap_may_still_discuss_it`, `tests/test_published_roadmap_status.py::test_the_section_the_site_lifts_is_still_there_and_not_empty` |
| G334 | **An empty list is how a widget spells "no issues".** `codequality` -- GitLab's Code Quality report, the third renderer of the gate's own report after the two SARIF builders -- emitted only `report["findings"]`, so an undetermined gate wrote `[]` and the widget told the reviewer the change was clean. G328 fixed this for `audit --format sarif` and G331 for `sarif_of_gate`; this is review class 21 twice removed and still one function along, in the same file. It now appends the gate's own undetermined line, one issue naming every rule that reached no verdict, and one per file that was not read or not audited -- reading `unreadable` under both `file` (the gate's key) and `path` (the audit's), and only those `skipped` rows carrying `forbidsVerdict`, because a Helm template or a file that is simply not a manifest is not a gap in the evidence and raising it would put an issue on every chart in the repository. All of them carry `severity: info` and never a blocker: the gate's exit code is what stops a pipeline, and a coverage gap raised as a defect is read as a false positive and gets the whole report dismissed. The rule for the next renderer: count the renderers of a report before fixing one of them | `tests/test_codequality_coverage.py::test_an_undetermined_gate_is_not_an_empty_report`, `tests/test_codequality_coverage.py::test_the_rules_that_reached_no_verdict_are_named`, `tests/test_codequality_coverage.py::test_a_file_that_was_not_read_is_one_issue_anchored_to_that_file`, `tests/test_codequality_coverage.py::test_every_issue_has_the_fields_gitlab_requires`, `tests/test_codequality_coverage.py::test_a_run_that_evaluated_everything_raises_no_coverage_issue`, `tests/test_codequality_coverage.py::test_a_skipped_file_that_does_not_forbid_the_verdict_is_not_raised` |
| G345 | **A rerun of an immutable upload must be a no-op, not a failure that strands the release.** `publish-pypi` ran `pypa/gh-action-pypi-publish` with no inputs, so a rerun after a partial upload hit a file PyPI already held and exited non-zero. `publish-mcp-registry`, `homebrew`, `brew` and `github-release` all wait on that job, so the recovery path for a half-finished release was the one path that could not be taken: PyPI would hold the wheel while the tag had no release page, no chart, no SBOM and -- from 0.10.0, which is the first release to attach one -- no `kubectl-noob-v<version>.tar.gz`, which is the asset `krew/noob.yaml` names, so the krew-index manifest would point at nothing. `docs/operations.md` records that shape twice (v0.7.1 and v0.8.0) and tells a maintainer not to delete or re-push the tag, which leaves rerunning the jobs as the only move. `skip-existing: true` makes it work: an upload of a file PyPI already serves is skipped rather than failed, and a release that got halfway can be finished from the run that got there. The flag is only about re-uploading the same version, so it cannot mask a wrong artefact: the version is new on every release, and `build-dist` still verifies the tag against `__version__` and the dists against the publish action's own twine before anything leaves the runner | `tests/test_distribution.py::test_a_rerun_of_the_pypi_upload_is_a_no_op_not_a_failure` |
| G344 | **A fixer that rewrites what the audit refuses to read is two sources of truth, and the one that writes wins.** `_fix_api_version` rewrote any `apiVersion` whose `kind` it recognised, so a kind this tool models under a group it does not serve -- somebody else's CRD that happens to share a name -- was treated as a typo in a built-in and rewritten. `projectcalico.org/v3 NetworkPolicy`, whose spec is a `selector` expression and an `ingress` of actions, became `networking.k8s.io/v1`; Knative's `serving.knative.dev/v1 Service` became core `v1`; Volcano's `Job` became `batch/v1`; and the Kubernetes documentation's own `stable.example.com/v1 CronJob` example became `batch/v1`. Each one was a VALID manifest turned into a broken one, and through `protocol`, where `apply_fixes` is the default, the broken core Service came back carrying a new HIGH finding of its own -- the tool manufacturing the problem it then reports. The audit had drawn this line correctly all along: `refusal` says "apiVersion names a group kube-noob does not know NetworkPolicy under" and withholds the verdict, so the two halves of one binary disagreed about the same object (class 9) and the writing half was the one with no guard. `_fix_api_version` now asks that same `refusal` before the rewrite and, when it answers, appends a `manual` line naming the reason and BOTH readings -- the served apiVersion if this is a typo, nothing to change if it is a custom resource of that name -- rather than choosing one of them. The guard is narrowed to a group no rule in this tool reads any kind under, read off the same `SUBJECT_GROUPS` table rather than a second list of what a group is, so `apps.openshift.io/v1 Route` stays on the other side of the line and is still corrected: `apps.openshift.io` serves `DeploymentConfig`, so a `Route` under it is a typo in the group of a real OpenShift kind, the spec survives the rewrite and the result is a valid manifest -- the repository's own `bad-bundle.yaml` carries that case and `tests/test_audit.py::test_fixer_resolves_blockers` expects it fixed, which is how the first, wider guard was caught. A wrong version inside a group this tool serves (`apps/v3 Deployment`, `networking.k8s.io/v1beta9 NetworkPolicy`, `extensions/v1beta1 Ingress`) is what `refusal` returns `None` for and is the same feature, so every genuine fix is unchanged. The rule for the next writer: a component that MODIFIES a manifest must not be more confident than the component that reads it, and where the reader withholds a verdict the writer withholds the edit | `tests/test_fixer_foreign_group.py::test_a_kind_under_a_group_this_tool_does_not_serve_is_left_alone`, `tests/test_fixer_foreign_group.py::test_the_reader_is_told_why_the_rewrite_was_declined`, `tests/test_fixer_foreign_group.py::test_a_typo_the_audit_refuses_is_still_fixed_when_the_group_is_one_this_tool_reads`, `tests/test_fixer_foreign_group.py::test_no_rule_reads_any_kind_under_these_groups`, `tests/test_fixer_foreign_group.py::test_a_wrong_version_inside_a_served_group_is_still_fixed`, `tests/test_fixer_foreign_group.py::test_the_fixer_and_the_audit_now_agree`, `tests/test_fixer_foreign_group.py::test_the_audit_does_not_refuse_a_genuine_typo`, `tests/test_fixer_foreign_group.py::test_the_fixed_output_is_still_the_same_document` |
| G349 | **A dependency bump arrived as four failing test legs instead of as a stale generated file.** `skill/kube-noob/SKILL.md` and `skill/kube-noob/scripts/kn.py` each carried a hand-written copy of three versions that `plugin/kube-noob/uv.lock` decides -- the install hint the model follows when pydantic, PyYAML or jsonschema is missing. Dependabot bumps the lock and nothing rewrote the copies, so one fact had three homes and two of them went stale silently (class 9). The only thing that noticed was one assertion inside the suite, which is the wrong place for it: `test (3.11)` through `test (3.14)` are the four slowest required checks in the repository, so a 16-to-59-minute run reported a one-line staleness that the generator gate could have named in under a second, and reported it as `AssertionError: pydantic` with no statement of which version was expected, which file was wrong, or what to run. `scripts/build_skill.py` now derives the three pins from the lock: `--write` rewrites both files, `--check` names the file, the version the lock pins, the version the file quotes and the command to run, and that check runs in `scripts/check.sh` and CI's `lint` job -- both of which finish before the suite starts. A file that quotes NONE of the three is a problem and not a pass, because a hint naming no version installs nothing; and a lock that pins no version for one of the three raises rather than defaulting, because a missing input must not read as agreement. The rewrite is a version substitution only, so the surrounding prose the model acts on is untouched and the line count is unchanged. This is what closed PR #50, whose bump was correct and whose four red legs were this defect reporting itself the long way round. The rule for the next writer: when dependabot owns a number, no second file may hold it by hand -- generate it, and let the gate that runs first say so | `tests/test_skill_pins_follow_the_lock.py::test_the_lock_is_the_only_place_these_three_versions_are_decided`, `tests/test_skill_pins_follow_the_lock.py::test_the_repository_as_committed_passes_the_pin_check`, `tests/test_skill_pins_follow_the_lock.py::test_a_lock_that_moved_without_the_skill_is_named_with_both_versions`, `tests/test_skill_pins_follow_the_lock.py::test_a_file_that_pins_nothing_is_a_problem_and_never_a_pass`, `tests/test_skill_pins_follow_the_lock.py::test_a_lock_missing_one_of_the_three_is_refused_and_not_defaulted`, `tests/test_skill_pins_follow_the_lock.py::test_write_rewrites_both_files_from_the_lock_and_then_changes_nothing`, `tests/test_skill_pins_follow_the_lock.py::test_rewriting_leaves_everything_but_the_version_alone`, `tests/test_skill.py::test_the_install_hint_pins_the_plugin_lock_versions` |
| G335 | **`satisfied` is the strongest word an assessment result says, so it has to mean the objective was evidenced.** On a report the gate itself called undetermined -- a privileged container in a `Deployment` under an apiVersion the audit refuses -- three objectives read `satisfied/pass`: CM-3(2), CM-6 and SA-22. CM-6 said satisfied on 6 clean rules with 27 that reached no verdict. The gap WAS stated, in the finding's `description`, which is prose a GRC tool does not parse while it reads `status.state`: review class 6, and the same shape G199 records two branches above (`--min-severity critical` turning a privileged container into a clean compliance record). Such a control is now EXCLUDED. `not-satisfied` was the other candidate and is wrong for the same reason `satisfied` is -- it asserts the objective is not met, and partial evidence does not say that either; an exclusion asserts nothing, and this document already had that mechanism for a control no rule ran over at all, so this extends it from no coverage to partial coverage. The discrimination that makes it usable: a rule that did not run because its SUBJECT is absent ("no Secret in the input") is this report's scope and not a gap in its evidence -- counting those as gaps took a clean input from 23 satisfied objectives to 1, and a compliance report that can never say satisfied is one nobody reads. A rule whose reason is not recorded counts as a gap, because an unknown must not read as a pass. A control with a finding, a withheld rule or an accepted exception is never excluded: a gap elsewhere does not withdraw what was found. The per-control counts are also emitted as `props`, so the coverage is machine-readable rather than only in the prose. The rule for the next state: the strongest word in a document needs the narrowest definition | `tests/test_oscal_partial_evidence.py::test_an_undetermined_report_claims_no_objective_satisfied`, `tests/test_oscal_partial_evidence.py::test_a_control_whose_evidence_was_not_gathered_is_excluded_not_failed`, `tests/test_oscal_partial_evidence.py::test_a_rule_whose_subject_is_absent_is_scope_and_not_a_gap`, `tests/test_oscal_partial_evidence.py::test_a_rule_with_no_recorded_reason_counts_as_a_gap`, `tests/test_oscal_partial_evidence.py::test_a_finding_is_still_reported_however_many_rules_went_unread`, `tests/test_oscal_partial_evidence.py::test_the_coverage_behind_an_objective_is_machine_readable` |
| G337 | **The admission webhook admitted what it could not evaluate, and said nothing.** `review`'s docstring already stated the policy -- "an input it cannot evaluate is admitted" -- and the policy is right: a fail-closed webhook that denies what it cannot read freezes the cluster, including the deploy that would fix it, which is what `failurePolicy` exists to trade. What was wrong is that the response was SILENT. `_findings` iterated `report.findings` and nothing else, so an object no rule reads produced an empty list and the API server admitted it with no warning at all -- at the one point in this product where a verdict gates a real action, a privileged container in a kind nobody modelled went in clean and the admin saw a blank response. It now reads `report.undetermined()` and appends one `warnings` entry naming what was withheld: non-blocking, already the channel used here for cross-object rules that could not be evaluated, already printed by `kubectl`. Said even when there are findings, because "this object has problems" and "part of it was never read" are independent statements and a reader who sees only the first concludes the rest was clean. A clean object still gets nothing, because a warning on every admitted object is one every admin turns off. This is the sixth renderer of one report to lose the same half of the answer, after the two SARIF builders, GitLab code quality, OSCAL and three MCP tools -- and the first one found by asking where to look rather than by tripping over it. The rule for the next one: enumerate the consumers of a report before fixing any of them | `tests/test_admission_says_what_it_withheld.py::test_an_object_the_audit_could_not_read_is_admitted_out_loud`, `tests/test_admission_says_what_it_withheld.py::test_a_clean_object_is_admitted_with_nothing_said`, `tests/test_admission_says_what_it_withheld.py::test_a_findable_problem_still_reports_as_a_finding_not_as_withheld`, `tests/test_admission_says_what_it_withheld.py::test_findings_and_a_withheld_part_are_both_said`, `tests/test_admission_says_what_it_withheld.py::test_the_warning_is_within_the_api_servers_length_guidance`, `tests/test_admission_says_what_it_withheld.py::test_an_operation_the_webhook_skips_is_unchanged` |
| G338 | **Count the renderers of a report before fixing one of them, and hold them to a predicate rather than to a precedent.** Seven renderers of the same audit report were each found, separately, to drop the half of the answer that says what was not evaluated: `audit --format sarif` (G328), `verify`, `verify_change`, `insight` and the CI wrapper (G329), `sarif_of_gate` (G331), `posture_scan`, `threat_model` and `fedramp_ksi` (G330), GitLab code quality (G334), OSCAL (G335) and the admission webhook (G337). Each was found by tripping over it and each fix taught one renderer one lesson, so the next renderer started from zero. `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py` enumerates the renderers out of the source with `ast` -- the public functions of `gate`, `oscal`, `admission` and `report_html`, and every MCP tool whose input carries `manifests` -- and holds each to one assertion over one fixture set: on a report whose verdict is withheld, the output states so AND its real consumer cannot read it as clean. Both halves, because G328 and G335 each ended with the gap stated in prose a consumer does not parse beside a field it does (review class 6), so the first half alone passes a renderer that is still wrong. The predicates are written as the consumer reads: for SARIF an empty or findings-only `runs[].results`, for GitLab `[]`, for OSCAL a `satisfied/pass` objective, for the webhook `allowed: true` with no warning, for the Markdown and text views a clean masthead with no limits section. A rule with no subject in the input is outside both predicates, because a renderer that reports a gap on every run is one every reader turns off -- the same discrimination G335 needed. A renderer with no adapter and no stated exemption fails `test_every_renderer_has_an_adapter`, which is the only test here that fails for something that has not happened yet. Putting them side by side found four defects nobody had tripped over, each recorded as a strict xfail so it fails the day it is fixed: `red_team` answers `RT-NODE-CRASH: mitigated` with `evidence: []` for an object no rule read while `RT-PRIVESC` in the same answer answers `undecided`; the gate's `incomplete` predicate counts `skipped` rows that forbid a verdict and not `unreadable` rows, so `verdict_word` says `passed` and `gate.markdown` writes "## kube-noob gate passed" for a run with a file it never read; OSCAL reports objectives `satisfied/pass` on that same run; and the HTML page and the OSCAL remark lose the file's NAME because both read `u["path"]` from rows a gate report keys on `file` -- the key mismatch G331 recorded, two renderers along. A Tekton `Task` with a privileged `docker:24-dind` step reached every renderer as a pass and no renderer was at fault: the detector is G333, and the fixture was checked against the report rather than declared, so the day that landed every adapter would be held to it. **Both predictions came true and this row is now history on those points.** G340-G343 fixed all four recorded defects, so every strict xfail started passing and failed -- which is what a strict xfail is for -- and the table was emptied in the same pull request rather than left behind. G333 landed too, so the `Task` is withheld, `withheld=True`, and the two tests that recorded the open gaps were deleted by their own instructions: `test_the_gate_predicate_does_not_count_a_file_it_could_not_read` (its docstring said the `unreadable` disjunct in `_withheld` "goes away when that test fails") and `test_a_kind_no_rule_reads_still_reaches_the_renderers_as_a_pass` ("this test then fails, which is the signal to delete it and set `withheld=True`"). A recorded defect that cannot expire is a second source of truth with no owner; each of these named the condition for its own removal, and that is why removing them was mechanical rather than a judgement call. | G356 | **kube-noob.com stated the previous release's facts for over two hours after every release, because the job that publishes it waited for a video render.** kube-noob.com is built from the latest GitHub release, and `release.yml`'s `site` job -- the dispatch that tells the site a release exists -- depended on `videos`. At v0.9.2 every job that actually publishes finished in **12 minutes** (`build-dist` 0.7, `publish-pypi` 0.4, `image` 4.2, `chart` 0.1, `chart-publish` 0.2, `publish-mcp-registry` 0.1, `homebrew` 0.2, `brew` 2.8, `github-release` 0.3) and `videos / render (walkthrough)` then ran for **128.7 more**. For that whole window the website served the old version string, the old rule counts and the old install commands, while PyPI, the image, the chart, the registry and Homebrew already carried the new ones -- the published artefacts and the page describing them disagreeing by a release. The dependency had a real reason once: a site built the moment the release existed published a Videos page saying the release had none, and 0.7.0 shipped exactly that page. kube-noob-site fixed that at its own end -- its deploy walks back through the last eight releases until one yields media, warns which release it fell back to, and never empties the page -- so the reason was gone and only this repository's comment still believed it. One fact about the website lived in two repositories, one of them was updated, and the stale half cost the entire render (class 9, across a repository boundary, which is the version of it no single test suite can see). `site` now runs on `github-release`; `site-videos` asks again once the renders land, and only when they succeeded, so a failed render leaves the first publish standing instead of reporting a publish of videos that do not exist. A `repository_dispatch` is never skipped at the other end -- kube-noob-site skips only its own schedule, and only when the product ref and the site sha both already match -- so the second ask really does rebuild. Both go through one reusable `site-publish.yml`, because two copies of a dispatch are two things that know how to publish the website and the one that drifts is the one nobody runs; the loud refusal on a missing `SITE_DISPATCH_TOKEN` is stated once, there. The rule for the next writer: before a job waits on another, check that the reason still holds at the other end -- a dependency is a claim about somebody else's behaviour, and claims go stale | `tests/test_the_site_does_not_wait_for_a_render.py::test_the_first_publish_does_not_wait_for_the_videos`, `tests/test_the_site_does_not_wait_for_a_render.py::test_the_website_is_asked_again_once_the_renders_land`, `tests/test_the_site_does_not_wait_for_a_render.py::test_both_asks_go_through_one_definition`, `tests/test_the_site_does_not_wait_for_a_render.py::test_each_ask_says_what_it_is_for`, `tests/test_the_site_does_not_wait_for_a_render.py::test_a_missing_token_fails_loudly_and_is_stated_once`, `tests/test_the_site_does_not_wait_for_a_render.py::test_the_reusable_workflow_is_bounded_and_read_only`, `tests/test_the_site_does_not_wait_for_a_render.py::test_no_release_job_lost_its_place_in_the_chain` |
| G358 | **kube-noob.com could not rebuild itself for six days and the watchdog was green for every hour of it.** Every scheduled deploy in `kube-noob-site` failed from 2026-11-17 to 2026-11-23 -- twelve completed runs, no successes -- on `ImportError: cannot import name 'render_text' from 'kube_noob.debug.diagnose'`. The site's generator had begun using a symbol that was on this repository's `main` and in no release, while the site builds from the latest RELEASE; `git tag --contains` puts that symbol first in v0.10.0, so the website was broken for six days **because the release was being held**, and the two facts that looked separate were one. `site-sync`'s `drift` job ran every six hours throughout and reported success every time. It is not broken: it asks whether the published site is behind the latest release, and the answer was no -- the site served v0.9.2 and v0.9.2 WAS the latest release. **Equality held by stagnation is indistinguishable from equality held by a working system**, so a site that could not build at all was invisible for exactly as long as nothing was released, and the workflow's own header claimed that "a deploy that breaks ... shows up as a red run here". It did not, twelve times. This is the project's defect in the project's own alarm, and the costly part is that the alarm measured the GAP between two numbers rather than the MECHANISM that keeps them equal. A new `deploys` job reads the site's own completed deploy conclusions and fails when the newest one failed, whatever the two versions say, naming the consecutive-failure count and the run to open; `success` and `skipped` are the only conclusions that pass. Three separate refusals come before any verdict, each with its own non-zero exit: no `SITE_DISPATCH_TOKEN` ("it is simply unknown"), a non-200 read ("SITE_DISPATCH_TOKEN needs Actions: read ... Not being able to look is not the same as nothing being wrong"), and an empty history ("an unanswered question") -- because a watchdog that cannot see the thing it watches has not found it healthy. `drift` is untouched: the at-or-past-release pass, the branch-build case and the grace period all stay, since this adds a question rather than weakening the one that was already right. The header now carries the measurement instead of the claim. | G357 | **`brew install kube-noob` served the previous release for a repository that said 0.10.0 everywhere else.** v0.10.0 reached PyPI, the chart, the MCP Registry and the GitHub release. The job failed loudly, which is correct and is the only reason this was visible at all, and then nothing closed the gap -- so the `brew tap kube-noob/kube-noob ...` and `brew install kube-noob` that the README, `docs/install.md` and the `public-tap` CI job all name kept installing **0.9.2**, with no sentence anywhere saying so.
