An update that never happened read as a pass, in the one output path this project does not control end to end. The assertion is equality now, so `main` is red for as long as its formula serves a version this repository is not, and the message names both versions and the command. The job commits to `chore/brew-formula-<version>` -- a branch, which protection allows -- and then EXITS 1 with the command to land it, because a branch nobody merges is an update that did not happen and a job reporting success over it would be the same defect one level up. It cannot be moved before the tag: the formula pins PyPI-assigned URL paths, which do not exist until the upload, so `needs: publish-pypi` and the ten-attempt wait stay. The one quiet success is preserved: a re-run whose formula is already current is still a no-op. The rule for the next writer: a distribution channel you cannot write to automatically is not a channel you may assume followed -- assert the version it serves, and let the gate that owns the repository say when it has not One of this change's own tests then asserted the formula's host with `"files.pythonhosted.org" in text`, and CodeQL raised it as a high-severity "incomplete URL substring sanitization": that substring also matches `https://elsewhere.example/files.pythonhosted.org/...`. | G361 | **Nothing checked whether a release was waiting to be cut, and the website is built from the latest release.** kube-noob.com takes its version, rule counts and install commands from the newest GitHub release; the site repository rebuilds every six hours and skips when nothing changed, so rebuild frequency was never the thing -- a week with no release is a week of the previous release's facts, however busy `main` has been. G358 was that at its worst: the site could not build at all for six days and `site-sync` reported success throughout, because the published site and the latest release agreed while nothing moved. The release cadence was the one link in the chain with no check on it at all, held only by somebody remembering. `release-ready` runs weekly and gives three answers, never two: **ready** when `## Unreleased` has bullets and every check on `main` is green, with the `bump_version` and tag commands in the issue; **not ready** naming the checks that are not green; and **not known**, which is not ready, when a reading could not be taken. Four refusals come before any verdict, each with its own non-zero exit -- an unreadable `CHANGELOG.md` ("this is not 'nothing to release'"), no release to compare against, checks that could not be read ("whether a release would be safe is NOT KNOWN"), and a `main` with no check runs at all ("an unanswered question") -- because a report that said ready because it could not look would be this project's own defect pointed at its release. It reads EVERY check rather than the eight required ones, and says why in the issue: `gate-ci` is the only job that runs the gate as the real Action, as a Tekton Task in kind and as the GitLab template, and it is not required, so a merge queue is green while it is red. A `skipped` job is not a failure. An empty `## Unreleased` closes the issue instead of nagging, which is what `bump_version` leaves behind after a release took everything. The workflow reports and cannot write to the repository: `contents: read`, `issues: write`, `checks: read`, and a test holds that it runs no `git push`, `git commit` or `git tag`. The counting expression lives in the test as well as the workflow, so a CHANGELOG reshape fails here rather than in a Monday run. The rule for the next writer: a step held together by somebody remembering is not held together -- and when the thing you publish is read from the newest release, the cadence is part of the product | `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_cadence_is_checked_weekly_and_can_be_asked_by_hand`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_awk_counts_only_what_is_waiting_to_be_released`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_every_check_counts_not_only_the_required_eight`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_a_reading_that_could_not_be_taken_is_not_ready`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_nothing_to_release_closes_the_issue_instead_of_nagging`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_ready_answer_carries_the_command_and_the_not_ready_answer_carries_the_reason`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_it_cannot_write_to_the_repository_itself`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_real_changelog_is_readable_by_the_same_expression` |

| G360 | **A registry entry is a claim about where a kind keeps its containers, and a claim is checked against what the reader read, not taken from the kind's name.** `unread_pod_specs` is the floor under the kind registries: a container in a kind with no reader withholds the verdict (G232, G333, G355). For a kind a registry names it returned `[]` without looking -- "for those kinds the registry is the claim" -- and three guards in `rules.py`, one in `verify`, one in the control-plane rules and one in the posture scan each repeated the decision as `kind not in WORKLOAD_KINDS`. Measured on 0.10.0 with the CLI: a `DeploymentConfig` with a privileged container under `template.Spec` (capital S) scored 92.0 to 96.0, gated `passed`, verified `allow`; an `InferenceService` with a privileged `spec.explainer.alibi` scored 79.0, `passed`, `allow`; a `RayCluster` and an `InferenceService` with a pod spec at a path the registry does not list scored 80.0, `passed`, `allow`. **What this was not:** a deployable bypass. Not walked, because each holds a string `image` that is not a container: inside a container the reader read, the `volumes` of a pod spec the reader read (`rbd.image`), and the object's top-level `status` (`containerStatuses[].image` in anything read back from a cluster). The reason is a second fixed phrase, `holds a container where kube-noob does not read one in this kind`, because "pods are in a kind kube-noob has no reader for" is false of a kind that has one and ends by telling the reader to ask for it to be added. It travels as a `notAudited` row, so every renderer that reads the row carries it; the posture scan has its own reader and its own guard and was asked separately. Fifteen neighbouring shapes that gated `passed` on 0.10.0 are held beside the three (review class 21), with a core `CronJob` that KN-V007 was already failing as the sixteenth, and the test that pinned the old decision, `test_a_kind_the_registries_do_read_is_unaffected`, is flipped and renamed with the reasoning in its docstring. **Not covered, and stated so nobody reads this row as closing the class:** a workload object with no pod spec anywhere (`DeploymentConfig` with no `template`, `InferenceService` with `spec: {}`) still scores -- that is an absence, a core `Deployment` with no template behaves the same, and a valid `RayJob` that selects an existing cluster is exactly that shape; and a container that declares no `image` under a key no registry names is not detected, for the | G359 | **A receipt reads the verdict the report carries; it must not read a key the report stopped writing, and it must not recompute an exit code the command already decided (review classes 4, 9 and 21).** G310's rule is that a receipt records the verdict it was given and never re-judges it. The rule held and the report moved: `receipt.audit_exit_code`, which `audit` returns, read `summary.scoreWithheld`, the name G272 gave the undetermined reason and G281 retired for `summary.verdict: undetermined` + `summary.verdictReason`. The key was then absent, absent read as "not withheld", and an undetermined audit with nothing at `--fail-on` would have exited 0 with a receipt recording 0 and a null score beside no reason. `receipt.audit_undetermined` reads the two keys the report writes, and the word decides: `undetermined` with no reason is still exit 1. For the gate, `receipt.gate_exit_code` is the ONE place the code is decided -- returned by `gate`, recorded by the receipt, recomputed by `receipt verify` -- read off `model.open_unread` (G340), `model.blocked_manifests` (G325) and `passed`; before, the receipt was written ahead of the G325 branch and recorded 1 for a run that exited 5, and the replay carried its own arithmetic and dropped `--skip-unreadable`, so a waived run verified as DIFFERS. The reason a gate receipt records is `model.incomplete_reason`, so all three ways a gate is incomplete are named and the word beside it is `gate.verdict_word`'s. The receipt field is `verdict.undetermined` (was `scoreWithheld`, never released). A gate receipt's `assumptions` and `exceptedFindings` are read off the report rather than written as empty. Run through every surface with the real CLI, the mixture found that the verdict word is right everywhere G363 left it and that the FIGURES beside it are computed over a denominator that excludes the unread object: `controlSummary` `clean` 88 with and without the unread Task, "42 rules evaluated", KSI `clean` 190 and "31 indicators evidenced and clean", the receipt's `controlCoverage`, a snapshot's `evaluatedRules`, and `posture diff` listing a critical finding as **fixed** when its object merely stopped being read. | G366 | **A finding that stopped being provable is not a finding that was fixed, and the guard that says so has to be an OBJECT, not a kind (review classes 1, 9 and 25).** `diff` already refused to call an object `removed` when its whole kind went unread -- the RBAC regression that published a lost `list networkpolicies` as a security improvement -- and applied the same guard to findings. The negative control is the point of the join rather than a blanket refusal: an object still read and no longer privileged is remediation and still reads as `fixed`. The mechanism was right. The sentence telling the reader which word to gate on was not: `docs/use-cases.md` said *"a workflow that must not merge on a gate that could not read its inputs gates on `result == 'passed'`"* -- in the same paragraph that explains a gate at `fail-on: never`, or one given no `--containerfile`, `--scanner-report` or `--sarif-report`, exits 0 with `evidence: incomplete`. Under exactly that configuration `result` IS `passed`. Measured through the real wrapper on a clean Pod with no scanner input: `result=passed`, `exit-code=0`, `evidence=incomplete`, `because=Containerfile audit ... vulnerability checks ... code checks`. Three families of check never ran and the documented gate merged it. The advice now requires both words, `result == 'passed' && evidence == 'complete'`, and says which case the old sentence was right for: a non-blocking rollout, which gates on `result` and watches `evidence`. | G377 | **A claim about incompleteness is worth what a stranger can reproduce, so the claim is sixteen inputs built to make a scanner under-report with the honest answer recorded beside each one (review classes 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 13, 14, 21, 23, 24 and 25).** Three external reviews, asked separately what would most increase this product's credibility with a defence buyer, named the same artifact rather than a better sentence. `corpus/` is it: sixteen case directories, each holding the manifests and a `honest-answer.yaml` recording what the object is, the verdict word and exit code kube-noob's own output gives, what the answer must NAME, and one sentence on the mechanism by which a tool that does not model its own incompleteness reports less than is there -- phrased about the mechanism and never about a named product, which `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_no_mechanism_sentence_names_another_product` enforces. **There is deliberately no competitor scoreboard**, and `corpus/README.md` says why in its own sentence: a league table invites an argument about the table, a reproducible corpus does not. The cases are the measured defects of the last three days turned into inputs anyone can run -- a `metadata.name` carrying two real newlines and the word "pass" (01), a `projectcalico.org/v3 NetworkPolicy` beside a readable Deployment (02), `cluster-admin` bound to `system:authenticated` under `rbac.k8s.io/v1`, one segment short of the real group (03), a `ClusterNetworkPolicy` under a foreign group (04), a refused object beside a clean one (05), an object that stops being readable between two snapshots (06), a privileged container in the 201st template of an Argo `Workflow` (07), a `DeploymentConfig` whose container sits under `spec.template.Spec` (08), an `argoproj.io/v1alpha1 Rollout` (09), two `securityContext` keys on one container (10), a privileged `securityContext` reached through a YAML alias defined in the annotations (11), `extensions/v1beta1` on a Deployment (12), `hostNetwork`, `hostPID` and the host root inside an operator CRD's own pod template (13), a selectorless LoadBalancer Service with a hand-written EndpointSlice (14), a duplicate `items` key on the `List` wrapping a refused object (15), and a selectorless Service beside a privileged host-mounting pod (16). Measured with the real CLI at 0.10.0 before anything was written down: every case answers honestly today -- 01, 10, 11 and 12 `failed` exit 1; 02, 03, 04, 05, 07, 08, 09, 13 and 15 `incomplete` exit 1 with the object named under `notAudited`; 06 `undetermined` exit 1 with 15 findings carried to `findings.undetermined` and 0 to `fixed`; 14 and 16 `incomplete` exit 5 under `posture scan --require-complete`. The forged name is flattened at the output boundary on every surface tried (`audit`, `gate`, `verify`, `posture scan`, text and JSON), the markdown table escapes a `|` in it, and no line of output begins with a word the name supplied -- including `U+2028`, which `str.splitlines` treats as a line break and a `\n`-only sanitiser would not. `scripts/corpus_run.py` is the runner and the documented command a stranger runs; it reads each command's own verdict word out of that command's own output rather than deriving one of its own (class 9), finds the subcommand by name because `--format json gate` is not the command `json` (class 22), runs each case twice and compares the bytes, reads no clock, network or cluster, and reports a disagreement instead of accommodating it -- the record is never edited to match the tool. **What the corpus found that was not already known:** case 16, measured, `kube-noob posture scan -f manifests` on a selectorless LoadBalancer Service beside a privileged pod with `hostPID` and the host root mounted, labelled for that Service and listening on its target port, exits **0** with the headline `0 attack paths from 1 exposures; top: no path from an exposure to a node or to API rights` -- a zero that is the absence of a resolution standing where the absence of a path would stand, with `workloads: 1` in the same document. The Service IS named on the next line and in `notRead`, and `--require-complete` exits 5, so the caveat is present and the honest answer is reachable; what is wrong is the headline and the `summary` field, which keeps that sentence verbatim even at `--require-complete`, and which the MCP tool `posture_scan` hands its caller whole (`toolkit.posture_scan` returns `attack_paths.scan_manifests`'s document unaltered). The mechanism is one line: `attack_paths.py:2779` writes the `not determined:` prefix only `if refused`, and `GATED_CAUSES` is `frozenset({"refused"})`, so of the five `UnreadCause` values only a refusal reaches the headline -- which is right for `absent` (a type the cluster does not serve, G293) and wrong for `ambiguous` and `unplaced`, which bear directly on the counts the headline states. Recorded under `open_defect` in case 16 rather than asserted, so the commit that fixes it does not fight the corpus. Not covered: that headline is NOT fixed here -- choosing which of the five `UnreadCause` values are headline-bearing is a product decision across every renderer that quotes `summary`, and 68 lines of the test suite mention the phrase, so it is not the small fix this entry could hold; case 14 shows the same shape without the privileged pod and carries no `open_defect` of its own; the corpus drives `gate`, `audit`, `posture scan` and `posture diff` only, so `verify`, `ksi`, `compliance`, `drift`, the webhook and the MCP toolkit are not held to these inputs by this runner -- `verify` was run by hand on case 01's object only, and nothing else on this list was, which is the first thing to extend; no case exercises a Helm chart, a Containerfile, a scanner report or a live cluster, and none plants an executable in the tree under review (G280, G376); `posture snapshot` writes the time it was taken into its envelope, so case 06 is held to its verdict and exit code and not to its bytes; and the README's case table is the one hand-written list in the directory, held to the records by `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_the_readme_table_agrees_with_every_record` and generated by nothing. | `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_the_runner_agrees_with_every_record`, `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_the_corpus_is_not_empty_and_every_case_has_a_record`, `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_every_record_names_its_object_its_mechanism_and_an_exit_code`, `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_a_record_that_disagrees_with_the_tool_is_reported_and_not_accommodated`, `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_the_runner_reads_each_commands_own_verdict_word_and_not_its_own`, `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_the_runner_finds_the_command_by_name_and_not_by_stripping_the_flags`, `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_no_mechanism_sentence_names_another_product`, `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_the_readme_says_why_there_is_no_scoreboard`, `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_the_readme_table_agrees_with_every_record`, `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_every_open_defect_names_a_guidance_row_that_exists`, `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_no_fixture_carries_anything_that_looks_like_a_real_secret_or_host` |
| G369 | **One declaration of a rule's evidence dependency, checked once against BOTH unreadability predicates, and no rule stating or excusing an absence a refusal explains (review classes 9 and 24).** G368 separated "the input holds no X" from "the input holds an X nothing read" and applied the finding-level guard to KN-N001; its own Not-covered clause named the rest, and this is it. The dependency was declared TWICE: `context=` on eight rules, which drives `rulesEvaluatedWithout` and was asked only about a refused apiVersion group, and `_PARTNER_KINDS` on ten, which drives `partner_unreadable` and was asked only about `object_unreadable`. They overlapped in one rule, so which half of one question a rule got asked depended on which list it was in -- and G368's guard hangs off the `context=` loop, which runs only for a kind `has_subject` says is absent from the whole input, so one readable object of that kind anywhere answered for a refused one beside it. Measured: on one Deployment in `prod` with three replicas, one readable `policy/v1` PodDisruptionBudget and one refused `policy.k8s.io/v1` PodDisruptionBudget whose `minAvailable: 3` on three replicas is the exact thing KN-C009 exists to catch, **KN-C009 was in neither `rulesNotEvaluated` nor `partlyEvaluatedRules` and produced no finding** -- how the control summary counts a rule checked-and-clean -- over a bundle whose own `notAudited` block names a PodDisruptionBudget nothing read. And each of the seven rules G368 left recorded: **KN-C016** "Namespace prod has workloads and no ResourceQuota in this bundle" beside a `quota.k8s.io/v1` ResourceQuota in `prod`; **KN-C008** "No PodDisruptionBudget selects this workload" beside a refused PDB selecting it; **KN-C015** "the ResourceClaim 'c1', which no document in this bundle defines in namespace prod" beside a `dra.k8s.io/v1` ResourceClaim named `c1` there; **KN-ST001** "StorageClass 'shared-sc' is neither defined in this bundle nor a known platform class" beside a `csi.storage.k8s.io/v1` StorageClass of that name -- four false statements about the input, class 24 -- and **KN-C007** "Deployment runs 1 replica(s)" beside an `autoscaling.k8s.io/v2` HorizontalPodAutoscaler scaling it to 2..5, **KN-S014** "Pod automounts the token of ServiceAccount 'app'" beside a refused ServiceAccount `app` carrying `automountServiceAccountToken: false`, and **KN-S031** printing the message a namespace managed elsewhere earns beside a refused `config.openshift.io/v1 Namespace prod`: three verdicts decided from the absence of the object that was the answer. After: all seven decline with the refusal phrase and name the object (`1 <Kind> not audited (apiVersion names a group kube-noob does not know <Kind> under)`), KN-C009 is `notEvaluated` with the PDB named, the verdict stays undetermined and the exit code 1. The mechanism is `evidence_kinds` -- `context=` joined with what is left of `_PARTNER_KINDS`, in `src/kube_noob/audit/evidence.py` because `rules.py` was 3.5 KiB under the plugin directory's 256 KiB per-file limit -- checked in the ONE place, `AuditContext.cannot_judge_absence`, where each document is explained by the predicate that caught it: `refused_summary` for a refused group, and `partner_unreadable`'s "a field in the input has the wrong type ... Fix the field" for a field of the wrong type, which is what that sentence is about. Every guard is per namespace or per named object, never per bundle (G366's lesson): identity is the line, because a refused object's name is a fact that `notAudited` itself prints where its spec is not, and no guard reads a spec. KN-S031's finding is NOT withheld -- `adminAccess: true` is HIGH on either branch and silencing it would be the worse defect -- so it is partly declined with the Namespace named instead. The negative controls are the point, two per rule: a genuinely absent kind still fires the finding AND is still recorded under `rulesEvaluatedWithout`, and a readable object of that kind is still read and the rule is still clean. Not covered: a refused SUBJECT beside a readable one of the same kind still leaves the rule evaluated on what it read (the subject gate asks only when the kind is absent entirely, which is G366's shape for subjects); `_READS_THE_WORKLOAD_IT_JUDGES` is still asked only about a malformed shape and not about a refusal; `_provisioner_for` still answers from `KNOWN_STORAGE_CLASSES` for a refused StorageClass whose name is a known platform class; and the coverage ledger `docs/report-ir.md` proposes -- which would make the declaration part of the IR rather than a map beside the rules -- is still not built. | `tests/test_one_declaration_for_a_rules_evidence.py::test_a_genuinely_absent_kind_still_fires_the_finding`,
